Initially, she channeled her hurt into overworking on freelance design projects and elaborate meal-prepping, but as months went by her social circle narrowed, she pulled back from the co-ed soccer league and gallery nights, and she spent more and more time alone in her apartment planning trips and creative projects she never actually started. Subtle changes in her thinking and mood were written off by friends as a reaction to the divorce until she abruptly withdrew from her art-education coordinator training program, missed rent payments despite having savings, and stopped showing up to community workshops she had organized. Nearly two years after the divorce, her ex-husband and a close friend convinced her to see a psychiatrist when she confided that she had quit all her freelance work because she felt that something was deeply wrong with her and that she no longer trusted her own mind.\n- Chief Complaint: “I feel like I’ve ruined everything in my life and I don’t deserve to be doing art or working with anyone anymore.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): For a long time now I've been convinced that the people at the museum and even my old soccer friends are quietly blacklisting me online, like there’s some shared document passing around saying I’m unstable or dangerous, and no matter how much my ex or my friend tells me that’s impossible, I can’t shake the feeling that every missed email or unreturned call proves they’re all in on it.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Pretty much every day, especially at night when the apartment is quiet, I hear this male voice that sounds a bit like my ex but harsher, telling me I’m a fraud, that my art hurts people and that I should just disappear, and it’s so clear and loud that I catch myself answering it out loud sometimes.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When I try to explain what’s been going on, my thoughts jump all over the place—I'll start telling you about missing rent, then suddenly I’m talking about colors in a painting or a recipe I never cooked, and I can tell people are lost but I can’t seem to line everything up in a straight story.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling like I’m already defeated before I even get out of bed, and it just sits on me all day so that even brushing my teeth feels pointless and I can’t imagine anything ever feeling OK again.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to live for gallery openings and pickup soccer and planning little art workshops, but now I just lie on the couch scrolling or staring at the ceiling, ignoring invites and messages because none of it seems worth the effort and I don’t get any joy out of it anymore when I do force myself to go.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): My appetite is all over the place—most days I just pick at toast or forget to eat until I’m dizzy, and then every so often I’ll suddenly binge on takeout late at night, but overall my clothes hang off me now because I’ve dropped so much weight without really trying.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): At night I lie there for hours with my mind spinning and that voice cutting in, and even when I finally crash I wake up at four or five in the morning with this jolt of dread and can’t fall back asleep, so I just stare at my phone or the ceiling until the sun comes up and I feel like I never actually rested.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): On bad days I move like I’m underwater—just sitting up feels like a chore, I shuffle slowly around the apartment, and when my friend asks me something on the phone there’s this long pause because it takes forever for the words in my head to turn into a sentence.\n- S029 (Fatigue): I feel completely drained all the time, like my body is made of sandbags, and even small things like showering or answering an email wipe me out so much that I have to lie down afterward.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I’m constantly replaying every mistake I’ve ever made—from marrying the wrong person to dropping out of the training program—and I honestly believe that I poisoned the spaces I worked in, that I let everyone down so badly I don’t deserve friends or a career in art anymore and should just stay out of people’s way as a kind of punishment.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): At least a few times a week I catch myself thinking it would be better for everyone if I just didn’t wake up, and on the really dark nights I find myself Googling painless ways to die and imagining exactly how I’d do it, even though I get scared and close the laptop before I actually make a plan.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_depressive_episode_occur_concurrently: When things got really bad after the divorce, I was already waking up hopeless and unable to do anything, and at the same time that accusatory male voice was talking to me and I was convinced people were blacklisting me, so the heavy depression and the paranoia have been tangled together most of the time.\n- depressive_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness_(Criterion_C): Looking back over the last couple of years, the stretches where I’ve felt that crushing sadness, worthlessness, and lack of motivation have taken up most of the time, with only brief, blurry periods where I wasn’t completely weighed down by it.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode_(Criterion_B): There was a period last summer when I didn’t feel especially sad or numb but was still hearing that harsh voice at night and was completely sure people were secretly sharing warnings about me, even though my mood had lifted a bit for a few weeks.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve basically stopped working or teaching, fallen behind on rent and bills even though I had some savings, and let most of my friendships and soccer and art communities fade because I either can’t keep up or feel too ashamed and paranoid to show up.\n- duration: This has been building and then steadily dragging on since the year after my divorce, so it’s been close to two years that I haven’t felt like myself.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The voice and the belief that people are secretly warning others about me started a few months after the worst of the divorce fallout and have been hanging around for well over a year now.\n- depressive_episode_duration: The deep, unshakable depression really settled in sometime midway through that first year after the divorce and has mostly stayed with me for well over a year.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English Initially, she channeled her hurt into overworking on freelance design projects and elaborate meal-prepping, but as months went by her social circle narrowed, she pulled back from the co-ed soccer league and gallery nights, and she spent more and more time alone in her apartment planning trips and creative projects she never actually started. (Psychomotor_Changes): On bad days I move like I’m underwater—just sitting up feels like a chore, I shuffle slowly around the apartment, and when my friend asks me something on the phone there’s this Subtle changes in her thinking and mood were written off by friends as a reaction to the divorce until she abruptly withdrew from her art-education coordinator training program, missed rent payments despite having savings, and stopped showing up to community workshops she had organized. Nearly two years after the divorce, her ex-husband and a close friend convinced her to see a psychiatrist when she confided that she had quit all her freelance work because she felt that something was deeply wrong with her and that she no longer trusted her own mind.\n- Chief Complaint: “I feel like I’ve ruined everything in my life and I don’t deserve to be doing art or working with anyone anymore.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): For a long time now I've been convinced that the people at the museum and even my old soccer friends are quietly blacklisting me online, like there’s some shared document passing around saying I’m unstable or dangerous, and no matter how much my ex or my friend tells me that’s impossible, I can’t shake the feeling that every missed email or unreturned call proves they’re all in on it.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Pretty much every day, especially at night when the apartment is quiet, I hear this male voice that sounds a bit like my ex but harsher, telling me I’m a fraud, that my art hurts people and that I should just disappear, and it’s so clear and loud that I catch myself answering it out loud sometimes.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When I try to explain what’s been going on, my thoughts jump all over the place—I'll start telling you about missing rent, then suddenly I’m talking about colors in a painting or a recipe I never cooked, and I can tell people are lost but I can’t seem to line everything up in a straight story.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling like I’m already defeated before I even get out of bed, and it just sits on me all day so that even brushing my teeth feels pointless and I can’t imagine anything ever feeling OK again.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to live for gallery openings and pickup soccer and planning little art workshops, but now I just lie on the couch scrolling or staring at the ceiling, ignoring invites and messages because none of it seems worth the effort and I don’t get any joy out of it anymore when I do force myself to go.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): My appetite is all over the place—most days I just pick at toast or forget to eat until I’m dizzy, and then every so often I’ll suddenly binge on takeout late at night, but overall my clothes hang off me now because I’ve dropped so much weight without really trying.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): At night I lie there for hours with my mind spinning and that voice cutting in, and even when I finally crash I wake up at four or five in the morning with this jolt of dread and can’t fall back asleep, so I just stare at my phone or the ceiling until the sun comes up and I feel like I never actually rested.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): On bad days I move like I’m underwater—just sitting up feels like a chore, I shuffle slowly around the apartment, and when my friend asks me something on the phone there’s this long pause because it takes forever for the words in my head to turn into a sentence.\n- S029 (Fatigue): I feel completely drained all the time, like my body is made of sandbags, and even small things like showering or answering an email wipe me out so much that I have to lie down afterward.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I’m constantly replaying every mistake I’ve ever made—from marrying the wrong person to dropping out of the training program—and I honestly believe that I poisoned the spaces I worked in, that I let everyone down so badly I don’t deserve friends or a career in art anymore and should just stay out of people’s way as a kind of punishment.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): At least a few times a week I catch myself thinking it would be better for everyone if I just didn’t wake up, and on the really dark nights I find myself Googling painless ways to die and imagining exactly how I’d do it, even though I get scared and close the laptop before I actually make a plan.\n▸ Additional at the same time that accusatory male voice was talking to me and I was convinced people were blacklisting me, so the heavy depression and the paranoia have been tangled together most of the time.\n- depressive_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness_(Criterion_C): Looking back over the last couple of years, the stretches where I’ve felt that crushing sadness, worthlessness, and lack of motivation have taken up most of the time, with only brief, blurry periods where I wasn’t completely weighed down by it.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode_(Criterion_B): There was a period last summer when I didn’t feel especially sad or numb but was still hearing that harsh voice at night and was completely sure people were secretly sharing warnings about me, even though my mood had lifted a bit for a few weeks.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve basically stopped working or teaching, fallen behind on rent and bills even though I had some savings, and let most of my friendships and soccer and art communities fade because I either can’t keep up or feel too ashamed and paranoid to show up.\n- duration: This has been building and then steadily dragging on since the year after my divorce, so it’s been close to two years that I haven’t felt like myself.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The voice and the belief that people are secretly warning others about me started a few months after the worst of the divorce fallout and have been hanging feeling like I’m already defeated before I even get out of bed, and it just sits on me all day so that even brushing my teeth feels pointless and I can’t imagine anything ever feeling OK again.\n- long pause because it takes forever for the words in my head to turn into a sentence.\n- S029 (Fatigue): I feel completely drained all the time, like my body is made of sandbags, and even small things like showering or answering an email wipe me out so much that I have to lie down afterward.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I’m constantly replaying every mistake I’ve ever made—from marrying the wrong person to dropping out of the training program—and I honestly believe that I poisoned the spaces I worked in, that I let everyone down so badly I don’t deserve friends or a career in art anymore and Nearly two years after the divorce, her ex-husband and a close friend convinced her to see a psychiatrist when she confided that she had quit all her freelance work because she felt that something was deeply wrong with her and that she no longer trusted her own mind.\n- Chief Complaint: “I feel like I’ve ruined everything in my life and I don’t deserve to be doing art or working with anyone anymore.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): For a long time now I've been convinced that the people at the museum and even my old soccer friends are quietly blacklisting me online, like there’s some shared document passing around saying I’m unstable or dangerous, and no matter how much my ex or my friend tells me that’s impossible, I can’t shake the feeling that every missed email or unreturned call proves they’re all in on it.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Pretty much every day, especially at night when the apartment is quiet, I hear this male voice that sounds a bit like my ex but harsher, telling me I’m a fraud, that my art hurts people and that I should just disappear, and it’s so clear and loud that I catch myself answering it out loud sometimes.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When I try to explain what’s been going on, my thoughts jump all over the place—I'll start telling you about missing rent, then suddenly I’m talking about colors in a painting or a recipe I never cooked, and I can tell people are lost but I can’t seem to line everything up in a straight story.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling like I’m already defeated before I even get out of bed, and it just sits on me all day so that even brushing my teeth feels pointless and I can’t imagine anything ever feeling OK again.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to live for gallery openings and pickup soccer and planning little art workshops, but now I just lie on the couch scrolling or staring at the ceiling, ignoring invites and messages because none of it seems worth the effort and I don’t get any joy out of it anymore when I do force myself to go.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): My appetite is all over the place—most days I just pick at toast or forget to eat until I’m dizzy, and then every so often I’ll suddenly binge on takeout late at night, but overall my clothes hang off me now because I’ve dropped so much weight without really trying.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): At night I lie there for hours with my mind spinning and that voice cutting in, and even when I finally crash I wake up at four or five in the morning with this jolt of dread and can’t fall back asleep, so I just stare at my phone or the ceiling until the sun comes up and I feel like I never actually rested.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): On bad days I move like I’m underwater—just sitting up feels like a chore, I shuffle slowly around the apartment, and when my friend asks me something on the phone there’s this But even though the heavy sadness had lifted, I was still hearing that harsh voice at night and remained absolutely convinced that people were secretly blacklisting me.\n\nDoctor: During the periods when your mood felt better and you were not depressed, did you experience any other unusual symptoms, such as seeing things that weren't there, feeling like people were monitoring you, or believing you had special powers or significance?\n\nPatient: I never felt like I had any special powers, but I definitely felt like people were monitoring and blacklisting me online. This crushing sadness has been there for the vast majority of that time, with only really brief, blurry moments where I didn't feel completely weighed down by it.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, have there been any periods lasting longer than two months where your mood improved significantly or you felt like your usual self?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any long breaks like that at all. Last summer there were maybe a few weeks where the heavy sadness lifted a little bit, but even then I didn't feel like my normal self. It's just been this constant, heavy weight for almost the whole time.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced other symptoms during this time, such as changes in your appetite, sleep patterns, or feeling constantly tired and low on energy?\n\nPatient: Yes, my appetite is all over the place—I barely eat and have lost a lot of weight, though sometimes I'll suddenly binge late at night. Sleep is awful too; I lie awake for hours with my mind spinning and then wake up at 4 AM with a jolt of dread. I feel completely drained all the time, like my body is made of sandbags and even showering wipes me out.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of worthlessness and the physical exhaustion been so severe that you have had thoughts of ending your life or felt that life is no longer worth living?\n\nPatient: Yes, quite often, especially on the really dark nights when I think it would just be better for everyone if I didn't wake up. I've even found myself Googling painless ways to die and imagining how I'd do it. It made functioning impossible—I ended up quitting my freelance work, falling behind on rent, and completely withdrawing from everyone. Last summer, there was a period of a few weeks where my mood lifted and I didn't feel particularly sad or numb, but I still heard that harsh voice at night and felt completely convinced people were sharing warnings about me.\n\nDoctor: During those weeks when your mood felt better, were you also experiencing other symptoms like disorganized thinking, a lack of motivation to do things, or a significant decrease in your ability to express emotions?\n\nPatient: No, during those weeks my motivation actually improved a bit and my thoughts didn't feel particularly jumbled or hard to express. But even though the heavy sadness had lifted, I was still hearing that harsh voice at night and remained absolutely convinced that people were secretly blacklisting me.\n\nDoctor: During the periods when your mood felt better and you were not depressed, did you experience any other unusual symptoms, such as seeing things that weren't there, feeling like people were monitoring you, or believing you had special powers or significance?\n\nPatient: I never felt like I had any special powers, but I definitely felt like people were monitoring and blacklisting me online. Even when my mood was a bit better, that harsh voice at night was still there telling me I was a fraud. It was just those same things persisting, even without the deep sadness.\n\nDoctor: During those weeks when your mood felt better, did you experience any periods of disorganized speech, a lack of emotional expression, or a significant decline in your ability to take care of your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: During those weeks last summer, my mood was definitely a bit better, but I still had the voice and that paranoia. As for having disorganized speech, a lack of emotional expression, or trouble with daily responsibilities, I'm not really sure or don't remember if I struggled with those during that specific brief period.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at those weeks last summer when your mood felt better, do you recall if the voice and the belief that you were being blacklisted occurred every single day, or were they present only occasionally?\n\nPatient: They were happening pretty much every day, not just occasionally. Even though the heavy sadness had lifted for a bit, I still heard that harsh voice every night and the constant feeling that I was being blacklisted didn't stop at all. Sleep is awful too; I lie awake for hours with my mind spinning and then wake up at 4 AM with a jolt of dread. I feel completely drained all the time, like my body is made of sandbags and even showering wipes me out.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of worthlessness and the physical exhaustion been so severe that you have had thoughts of ending your life or felt that life is no longer worth living?\n\nPatient: Yes, quite often, especially on the really dark nights when I think it would just be better for everyone if I didn't wake up. I've even found myself Googling painless ways to die and imagining how I'd do it. But I always get scared and close the laptop before actually making any kind of plan.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, have there been times when your mood and energy were so severely low that you were unable to function at all, perhaps even losing touch with reality or needing hospitalization?\n\nPatient: Yes, there were times when the crushing sadness and complete lack of energy happened right alongside hearing that harsh voice and feeling convinced I was being blacklisted. It made functioning impossible—I ended up quitting my freelance work, falling behind on rent, and completely withdrawing from everyone. I've never been hospitalized, but during those times I felt entirely shut down and disconnected from reality.\n\nDoctor: When you heard that voice and felt convinced you were being blacklisted, did those experiences occur only during your most severe episodes of depression, or did they happen at other times as well?\n\nPatient: They actually happened even when I wasn't feeling deeply depressed. Last summer, there was a period of a few weeks where my mood lifted and I didn't feel particularly sad or numb, but I still heard that harsh voice at night and felt completely convinced people were sharing warnings about me.\n\nDoctor: During those weeks when your mood felt better, were you also experiencing other symptoms like disorganized thinking, a lack of motivation to do things, or a significant decrease in your ability to express emotions?\n\nPatient: No, during those weeks my motivation actually improved a bit and my thoughts didn't feel particularly jumbled or hard to express. But even though the heavy sadness had lifted, I was still hearing that harsh voice at night and remained absolutely convinced that people were secretly blacklisting me.\n\nDoctor: During the periods when your mood felt better and you were not depressed, did you experience any other unusual symptoms, such as seeing things that weren't there, feeling like people were monitoring you, or believing you had special powers or significance?\n\nPatient: I never felt like I had any special powers, but I definitely felt like people were monitoring and blacklisting me online. Even when my mood was a bit better, that harsh voice at night was still there telling me I was a fraud. It was just those same things persisting, even without the deep sadness.\n\nDoctor: During those weeks when your mood felt better, did you experience any periods of disorganized speech, a lack of emotional expression, or a significant decline in your ability to take care of your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: During those weeks last summer, my mood was definitely a bit better, but I still had the voice and that paranoia. As for having disorganized speech, a lack of emotional expression, or trouble with daily responsibilities, I'm not really sure or don't remember if I struggled with those during that specific brief period.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at those weeks last summer when your mood felt better, do you recall if the voice and the belief that you were being blacklisted occurred every single day, or were they present only occasionally?\n\nPatient: They were happening pretty much every day, not just occasionally. Even though the heavy sadness had lifted for a bit, I still heard that harsh voice every night and the constant feeling that I was being blacklisted didn't stop at all. It was really unsettling because those things just wouldn't leave me alone.\n\nDoctor: Did these daily experiences of hearing the voice and feeling blacklisted persist for at least two weeks while you were not experiencing any significant mood symptoms?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely lasted for at least two weeks. During that period last summer, my mood had lifted and I didn't feel especially sad or numb, but I was still hearing that harsh voice every night and remained convinced that people were sharing warnings about me online."

I've never been hospitalized, but during those times I felt entirely shut down and disconnected from reality.\n\nDoctor: When you heard that voice and felt convinced you were being blacklisted, did those experiences occur only during your most severe episodes of depression, or did they happen at other times as well?\n\nPatient: They actually happened even when I wasn't feeling deeply depressed. Last summer, there was a period of a few weeks where my mood lifted and I didn't feel particularly sad or numb, but I still heard that harsh voice at night and felt completely convinced people were sharing warnings about me.\n\nDoctor: During those weeks when your mood felt better, were you also experiencing other symptoms like disorganized thinking, a lack of motivation to do things, or a significant decrease in your ability to express emotions?\n\nPatient: No, during those weeks my motivation actually improved a bit and my thoughts didn't feel particularly jumbled or hard to express. But even though the heavy sadness had lifted, I was still hearing that harsh voice at night and remained absolutely convinced that people were secretly blacklisting me.\n\nDoctor: During the periods when your mood felt better and you were not depressed, did you experience any other unusual symptoms, such as seeing things that weren't there, feeling like people were monitoring you, or believing you had special powers or significance?\n\nPatient: I never felt like I had any special powers, but I definitely felt like people were monitoring and blacklisting me online. As for having disorganized speech, a lack of emotional expression, or trouble with daily responsibilities, I'm not really sure or don't remember if I struggled with those during that specific brief period.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at those weeks last summer when your mood felt better, do you recall if the voice and the belief that you were being blacklisted occurred every single day, or were they present only occasionally?\n\nPatient: They were happening pretty much every day, not just occasionally. Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nYes, my appetite is all over the place—I barely eat and have lost a lot of weight, though sometimes I'll suddenly binge late at night.
